People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Differential includes persistent delusional disorder (F22.0), acute polymorphic psychotic disorder (F23.2), paranoid schizophrenia (F20.0), and paranoid personality disorder (F60.0) pending further assessment of symptom duration, other psychotic features, and functional impact.",

It's real.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, when you hear that man's voice telling you to 'check it again,' do you ever wonder if that voice might be coming from your own mind, or are you completely certain it's someone else speaking to you from outside your head?\n\nPatient: I don't... it's not my mind. I really don't know. Like I said, I can't remember exactly how it started. I wasn't... I wasn't keeping track of the days back then. I think... maybe it was around the same time. I just remember it all happening. I didn't keep track of which came first.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, I know you've said you're not entirely sure about the timing. My supervisors actually took me off the forklift. And some of the other safety stuff we do. They said my counts and... my routines were slowing the line down. \n\nAnd at home... I don't know. My husband just complains. He says he can't rely on me the way he used to. So... yeah. I guess it's been a little hard.\n\nDoctor: When you first started noticing that people at work were watching you and changing the pallet counts eight months ago, had you already been hearing that man's voice before that started, or did the voice start around the same time?\n\nPatient: I... I think it was around the same time. The voice and... noticing what they were doing with the pallets and watching me. It's all been going on for about eight months now.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to eight months ago, did the voice and the feeling that people were watching you start suddenly—like over just a few days—or did they gradually build up over a longer period of time?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess... maybe it just sort of happened. I can't really remember if it was all at once or not. It's just been going on for a while.\n\nDoctor: When you think about those first few days or that first week when you started noticing the voice and the watching—did it all come on very suddenly, like within a day or two, or was it more gradual over several weeks before it really became noticeable?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I said, I can't remember exactly how it started. I wasn't... I wasn't keeping track of the days back then. It just sort of became how things are.\n\nDoctor: In the months before you first started hearing that voice and noticing people watching you, did you notice any changes in yourself? I already told you, I don't really know how fast it all started. I just know the voice and... the feeling of being watched, people messing with things... it's been going on for about eight months. That's all I know.\n\nDoctor: Looking back to when this all started around ten months ago, when you first heard that voice or felt like people were watching you—did it happen suddenly, like you woke up one day and it was there, or did it come on gradually over weeks where it got worse and worse?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know how else to answer that. Like I just said, I don't know if it was sudden or slow. I wasn't paying attention like that. I didn't just wake up and write it down, it just... started. That's all I remember.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned things changed after you applied for the supervisor role about ten months ago. From the day you applied for that position until the day you clearly remember hearing that man's voice for the first time—was that a matter of days, or was it weeks or months in between?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know. It's just a blur. I applied for the job, and then... sometime later, the voice was there. I don't know exactly how long it was between the two.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask this a different way. When you first heard that man's voice telling you to check it again, had you already been feeling like people were watching you for a while before that, or did you start hearing the voice and feeling watched around the same time?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I think... maybe it was around the same time. I just remember it all happening. I didn't keep track of which came first.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, I know you've said you're not entirely sure about the timing. But thinking back as best you can—from when you first noticed something was off to when the voice and the feeling of being watched became really clear and unmistakable, would you say that happened over the course of just a few days, or did it take several weeks for it to build up to that point?\n\nPatient: I... I really can't say. I just... I don't remember it like that. It's too hard to think back that far and put a timeline on it. I just know it happened.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, when you first started experiencing all of this—the voice and the feeling of being watched—did it feel like something that came on you suddenly and intensely, or did it feel more like something that crept up on you slowly over time?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know how to explain it. Like I said, it's all just a blur. I can't really tell you if it was fast or slow. It just... it was just happening. I really don't know.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, I understand it's hard to remember exactly. But if you had to choose—when this all started, did it feel more like something that hit you suddenly and intensely over just a few days, or more like something that slowly crept up on you over many weeks?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't want to guess. I don't know. It's just... it's too much to try and figure out right now. I don't know which one it was.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, when you first started hearing that voice and feeling watched, did it feel shocking or surprising to you—like something completely new and unexpected—or did it feel more like you were just noticing something that had already been happening around you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I wasn't... I don't think I was paying attention to how it felt. It was just there. I really can't explain it any better than I already did.\n\nDoctor: Nicole, I know you've had trouble pinpointing the exact timing.
